<<<SUMMARY>>>
Chevron (CVX) is a highly leveraged bet on crude oil prices and geopolitical risk in the Strait of Hormuz. The stock has declined from $214.71 to $188 as oil prices fell 20% on ceasefire hopes between the U.S. and Iran. While CVX offers a fortress dividend with 39 years of consecutive growth and a reasonable 14x forward P/E multiple, its earnings are heavily dependent on oil prices remaining elevated. The stock faces a binary outcome: if the ceasefire holds, crude falls and CVX declines further; if talks collapse, crude spikes and CVX rallies significantly. Notable headwinds include Berkshire Hathaway's 35% stake reduction and insider selling.

Recent declines from $214 to $188 reflect falling oil prices on ceasefire optimism. Negative signals include Berkshire Hathaway's 35% stake reduction and $200M net insider selling over 12 months. The investment thesis hinges entirely on oil price direction, creating significant uncertainty.
XOM | neutral | Mentioned as a comparative peer that is larger and more diversified across chemicals and downstream operations, making it less oil-levered than Chevron. In falling-crude environments like the current one, ExxonMobil tends to outperform Chevron due to lower oil sensitivity, but the article does not provide specific analysis or price targets for XOM.

headline: Schwab's Dividend ETF's Worst Year Since 2012 Was a 5.5% Loss. The Cost Showed Up in the Good Years.
<<<SUMMARY>>>
The Schwab U.S. Dividend Equity ETF (SCHD) had only three losing years between 2012-2025, with its worst loss being 5.5% in 2018. While this defensive positioning protected it during market downturns like 2022 (down 3% vs S&P 500 down 18%), it significantly underperformed in rising markets. Over 14 years, $10,000 in SCHD grew to ~$49,000 compared to ~$70,500 in the S&P 500, demonstrating the trade-off between downside protection and upside participation.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
SCHD | neutral | The ETF demonstrates a clear trade-off: excellent downside protection with minimal losses in bad years (worst loss 5.5%), but significant underperformance in bull markets (22% vs 86% S&P 500 return over 2023-2025). Suitable for conservative income investors but lags in rising markets.
AMZN | neutral | Mentioned as a major 2022 loser (down ~50%) that was excluded from SCHD due to lack of dividend payments, illustrating why the dividend fund avoided significant losses that year.
TSLA | neutral | Noted as a 2022 major loser (down ~65%) excluded from SCHD due to no dividend payments, contributing to the fund's outperformance that year.
META | neutral | Cited as a 2022 significant loser (down ~64%) excluded from SCHD due to lack of dividend payments, exemplifying the fund's defensive positioning.
TXN | positive | Listed as SCHD's largest holding, up almost 70% in 2026, contributing to the fund's recent outperformance in the current year.
CVX | positive | One of SCHD's top 10 holdings, gained over 40% in 2026, with energy stocks representing ~14% of the fund and driving recent outperformance.

<<<SUMMARY>>>
ConocoPhillips is positioned as a strong earnings-beat candidate for Q3 2026 with a positive Earnings ESP of +4.50% and Zacks Rank #1, expecting $2.97 EPS (up 84.5% YoY) and $18.24B in revenues (up 17.5% YoY). NFG | negative | Negative Earnings ESP of -1.74% combined with Zacks Rank #4 (Sell) makes it difficult to predict an earnings beat. EPS expected to decline 5.7% YoY, and recent estimate revisions are slightly negative (down 0.5%), suggesting weakening earnings prospects.
